ASX — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • Customers or their foundries ship finished wafers to ASE on consignment, ASE turns them into packaged, tested parts, and ships them onward.
  • There is no disclosed contract duration, no escalator, no take-or-pay, no minimum volume and no backlog anywhere in ten years of 20-Fs.
  • Blended factory utilization moved from roughly 65% expected for 1Q25 to 80-85% in 2Q26, and management's own published mapping puts 70% utilization at a 24% ATM gross margin and about 85% at 30% (UBS fireside, 2025-12-03).
  • one customer accounted for NT$161,282,046 thousand of FY2025 operating revenues, 25.0% of the group, spanning the packaging and EMS segments and including OEMs manufacturing on its behalf
  • Between FY2023 and FY2025 the segment added NT$51,537m of revenue and NT$52,031m of it came from the bump/flip-chip/WLP/SiP line; wirebonding subtracted NT$764m.
  • Its revenue is disclosed only on earnings calls: US$250m in 2023, "over USD 600 million, accounting for around 6% of ATM revenues" in 2024, "$1.6 billion, accounting for 13% of ATM revenue" in 2025, guided above US$3.5bn for 2026 and "tracking ahead" of that, with management "aiming to double our LEAP revenue in 2027."
  • up-cycle incremental gross margins in this segment historically ran 39-50%. FY2025 delivered 25.6% at the gross line and 4.5% at the pre-tax line on NT$46,611m of extra revenue, the worst conversion of the decade in the year revenue grew fastest.
  • The first half of 2026 shows the reversal: ATM revenue rose from NT$179,233m (1H25) to NT$238,582m with gross margin moving 22.2% to 26.7%, an incremental gross margin of 40.2% and an incremental operating margin of 31.2%.
  • Testing sells machine time. Revenue equals installed testers times utilization times realized revenue per tester-year, and ASE discloses the first term only.
  • Over FY2018–FY2025 wafer probing compounded at 19.9% a year while final testing compounded at 5.8%. The shift toward probe did not begin with AI; it began in 2018 and AI accelerated it.
  • The marginal machine costs roughly twice what the average installed machine cost and earns at most 1.77x as much.
  • The reported revenue line is therefore roughly four-fifths a restatement of what USI bought from its suppliers, and the informative series is value-added revenue, meaning revenue less raw material.
  • FY2025's headline decline of 5.2% is five times the underlying contraction: value-added revenue declined 1.0% and gross profit 2.9%.
competitive-advantage
  • The advantage is real, it sits in cost position and allocation priority rather than pricing power, and it has not yet reached the capital line.
  • Against Amkor, the only other listed pure-play OSAT of comparable technology reach, ASE's assembly-and-test operations earned a 12.1% pre-tax margin in FY2025 against 6.6%, and a 23.5% gross margin against 14.0%.
  • Estimated packaging ROIC was 8.6% in FY2025 excluding working capital, against Amkor's 9.5% computed the same way, and the packaging figure was below Amkor's in four of the last five years once the SPIL merger goodwill is included.
  • The margin follows the loading, which is what a capacity business does and what a business protected by network effects or switching costs does not.
  • Against the three calibration points, this is the middle one: excess returns today at the top of the cycle, defensible for now, open to erosion as rivals, customers and the foundry partner shift.
  • Testing earns excess returns only at the top of the cycle, and the evidence does not support a durable structural advantage.
  • ASE's testing segment is roughly 2.1x King Yuan Electronics by revenue and earns a lower operating margin, 22.2% in FY2025 against 25.8%, and the gap has moved 8.3 points against ASE since FY2021.
  • Being the biggest packager did not deliver the highest-value test insertions on the highest-value chips in the industry's biggest cycle.
  • EMS has no durable competitive advantage. It earns approximately its cost of capital, it does not set price, and its position relative to the four US-listed contract manufacturers has deteriorated over five years.
  • In FY2020 ASE's EMS unit had the highest gross margin and the highest operating margin of this five-company set. In FY2025 it has the lowest operating margin.
  • Amkor is participating at a similar rate of improvement, which is what happens when the driver is industry loading rather than a company-specific edge.
  • The 20-F also states plainly that "most of our customers obtain services from more than one source."
competitive-landscape
  • It includes roughly US$8.4bn of USI contract-electronics revenue at an 8.9% gross margin that does no semiconductor packaging at all, while every other player in the table is measured on OSAT revenue only.
  • The correction does not change who is first; it changes the structure from "one dominant player at 45% plus a long tail" to a leader near 32%, a credible number two near 18% and a Chinese number three near 14%.
  • The share that moved in 2025 moved from Amkor to ASE, not from China to ASE. JCET, Tongfu and Tianshui Huatian went from 31.2% of top-ten revenue in 2024 to 31.2% in 2025, growing 13.7% in aggregate against a market that grew 13.6%. ASE grew 20.7%; Amkor grew 6.2%.
  • Nobody in this industry sets price.
  • ASE is the scale leader on total outsourced packaging revenue, at 1.6-1.7x Amkor's packaging-only revenue and roughly 2x JCET's
  • Any statement that ASE leads advanced packaging is false unless explicitly scoped to the merchant layer.
  • ASE's largest relationship by a wide margin does not buy its test services at a material level.
  • ASE's EMS is a sub-scale participant in a market whose leader is 31x larger, and it spent the largest demand event in EMS history losing relative position.
  • ASE's fastest-growing business is served at the allocation of a partner that is also the largest competitor in that business.
critical-points
  • ATM was 66% of 2Q26 revenue and 94% of operating profit, up from 61% and 87% a year earlier, so an adverse re-allocation lands on the highest-margin, highest-capex part of the business.
expectations
  • It is that consensus requires a full-year ATM gross margin of roughly 30.6% in FY2027 and 32.4% in FY2028, above the top of a range management has touched in one quarter four years ago and has twice declined to raise, on a utilization map management published itself that puts 30% at full capacity.
  • ASE systematically under-guides its semiconductor business and guides EMS roughly straight.
  • heavy capex buys revenue and not returns: across 39 OSAT capex-surge events with a three-year outcome, ROIC was higher three years later in 15 cases (38%), the median change was −0.8 points, and the median revenue CAGR over the following three years was +14.7%
  • ASE's own median of 0.78 of incremental revenue per unit of three-year capex is the best in its reference class by a wide margin, against a class median of 0.37, and consensus implies 0.73.
m-and-a
  • Eight years and roughly NT$160bn of cumulative cash later, consolidated operating margin has not durably exceeded the pre-deal level
management-claims
  • The advanced share of packaging revenue rose from 41.2% (2021) to 59.6% (2025), 18.4 points, while segment gross margin declined 3.6 points.
  • The correct characterisation is a large, cash-generative, fully depreciated annuity on trailing-edge unit volume, with revenue per tool still 21% below its FY2021 peak.
  • ASE is the largest and best-capitalised executor of the AI packaging build-out; the architecture-level leadership is TSMC's, and management says so.
  • The range has functioned as a floor claim in the downturn and a ceiling explanation in the upturn.
  • LEAP is also undefined in any filing, and its FY2026 target moved US$1.6bn to US$2.6bn to US$3.2bn to US$3.5bn to "ahead of 3.5" in nine months; without a definition, the split between demand and reclassification is not observable.
secular-trends
  • Every dollar of ATM growth over FY2022 to FY2025 came from the computing end market; communications shrank, and the automotive, consumer and industrial third of the business was flat in absolute terms.
  • The unit-driven part of ASE's business tracks wafer area, which grew 5.8%. ASE ATM grew 23.7% in US dollars. The 18-point gap is content per wafer plus price plus mix, not volume.
  • More than half of the 2026 market by dollars sits outside ASE's addressable base.
  • The claimed secular driver did appear in the numbers, and it did not account for a majority of the growth in either year. In FY2025 LEAP contributed 41% of the ATM revenue increment and the mainstream business 59%; on the FY2026 guide the split is roughly even.
  • The mainstream half is an industry-wide cyclical recovery rather than ASE share gain.
  • Management set the benchmark itself, twice, and has not met it.
technology
  • The FY2025 20-F defines the whole leading-edge category by process rather than by invention, and every architecture in its table has a shipping rival
  • roughly 66% is on-substrate work, meaning discrete back-end steps allocated out of a foundry partner's CoWoS flow; about 19% is wafer probe; under 10% is ASE's own architecture end to end; about 6% is final test
  • The financial record supports the first description: ASE's own end-to-end leading-edge line was roughly 1–2% of ATM revenue in FY2025–26.
  • at a 100 × 100 mm package only about seven modules fit on a 300 mm wafer, and at five to six reticle sizes utilization drops to 57%; a rectangular panel raises that to about 87% (UBS fireside)
  • In a business where utilization is the entire margin, moving a device to whichever tester is free is worth more than any package architecture in the portfolio, and ASE has disclosed it in the same language for a decade.
  • Total patents held went 6,433 (2024-01-31) to 6,433 (2025-01-31) to 6,358 (2026-01-31), and pending applications from 2,351 to 2,249, while R&D expense rose 14.0% to NT$32,852m and R&D headcount reached 14,258.
  • Amkor names "copper hybrid bonding" as a development programme in its FY2025 10-K; the phrase appears nowhere in ASE's FY2023–FY2025 20-Fs or in 24 earnings calls.
value-chain
  • ASE sits at the layer with the highest physical leverage over throughput and the lowest capture of the end price. It converts a bare die worth thousands of dollars into a saleable part for a fee that is a low single-digit percentage of that part's selling price, and it does so without owning the die.
  • Gross margin across the chain is bimodal. Design and foundry earn 60–71%. The physical-conversion layers, substrate at 24.8% and OSAT at 27.3% and 16.8%, earn a quarter to a third of that.
  • The alleged bottleneck owner and the alleged victim earn the same return, which argues against treating substrate scarcity as a rent the substrate makers are currently extracting from ASE.
  • The customer's return on buying ASE's service is therefore insensitive to ASE's price. A 20% increase on that layer costs the customer well under 1.5% of chip selling price.
  • Substrate is the structural constraint; equipment lead times and building construction are the operative ones in 2026.

Customers or their foundries ship finished wafers to ASE on consignment, ASE turns them into packaged, tested parts, and ships them onward.

business-explainer · business-model · ↩ back to dossier

Company

earnings callqualifies
As production resources get tighter, it needs to be emphasized that customers prefer manufacturing certainty. They like to know precisely the timing and pricing of wafers, substrates, packaging and testing services.
— Kenneth Hsiang, ASE Technology Holding Q1 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-04-29
filingsupports
The principal raw materials used in our packaging processes are interconnect materials such as leadframes and substrates, gold wire and molding compound. The silicon die, which is the functional unit of the semiconductor to be packaged, is supplied in the form of silicon wafers. Each silicon wafer contains a number of identical dies. We receive the wafers from the customers or the foundries on a consignment basis. Consequently, we generally do not incur inventory costs relating to the silicon wafers used in our packaging process.
— Advanced Semiconductor Engineering, Inc. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2016, 2017-04-21
filingsupports ✦ best
The principal raw materials used in our packaging processes are interconnect materials such as leadframes and substrates, gold wire, and molding compound. The silicon die, which is the functional unit of the semiconductor to be packaged, is supplied in the form of silicon wafers. Each silicon wafer contains a number of identical dies. We receive the wafers from customers or foundries on a consignment basis. Consequently, we generally do not incur inventory costs relating to the silicon wafers used in our packaging process.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
In the case of semiconductor packaging, the cost of the silicon die, typically the most costly component of the packaged semiconductor, is usually not reflected in our costs (or revenues) since it is generally supplied by our customers on a consignment basis.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
The Group identifies the contracts with customers, allocates transaction prices to performance obligations and, when performance obligations are satisfied, recognizes revenues at fixed amounts as agreed in the contracts with taking estimated volume discounts into consideration. ... When customers control goods while the packaging and testing are in progress, the Group measures the progress on the basis of costs incurred relative to the total expected costs as there is a direct relationship between the costs incurred and the progress of satisfying the performance obligations. Revenue and contract assets are recognized during the packaging and testing process and contract assets are reclassified to trade receivables when the packaging and testing are completed or when the goods are shipped upon customer's request.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
We offer a broad range of semiconductor packaging and testing services, and we offer EMS through USI Group. In addition to offering each service separately, we also offer turnkey services, which include integrated packaging, testing, and direct shipment of semiconductors to end users designated by our customers and solution-based proactive original design manufacturing.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
We offer drop shipment services for shipment of semiconductors directly to end users designated by our customers. Drop shipment services are provided mostly in conjunction with our testing services. We provide drop shipment services to a significant percentage of our testing customers. A substantial portion of our customers at each of our facilities have qualified these facilities for drop shipment services. Since drop shipment eliminates the additional step of inspection by the customer before shipment to the end user, quality of service is a key consideration.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingqualifies
For the majority of our testing equipment, we typically base our purchases on prior discussions with our customers about their forecast requirements. The balance consists of testing equipment on consignment from customers, which is dedicated exclusively to the testing of these customers' specific products. ... In cases where a customer has specified testing equipment that was not widely applicable to other products that we test, we have required the customer to furnish the equipment on a consignment basis.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01

Peer: Amkor Technology

filingsupports
The packaging and test services we provide occur subsequent to wafer fabrication, and the wafers that we receive from our customers are generally consigned to us. ... The silicon wafer is generally consigned from our customer. We generally do not take ownership of the customer consigned wafer, and title and risk of loss remains with the customer for these materials. Test materials constitute a very small portion of our total test cost. Generally, we purchase materials based on Amkor's commitments to customer forecasts, and our customers are generally responsible for any unused materials we purchase based on such commitments.
— Amkor Technology, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20

Peer: ChipMOS Technologies

filingsupports
In general, particular semiconductors can be tested using a limited number of specially designed testers. As part of the qualification process, customers will specify the machines on which their semiconductors may be tested. ... If a customer specifies test equipment that is not widely applicable to other semiconductors we test, we require the customer to furnish the equipment on a consignment basis.
— ChipMOS Technologies Inc. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-14

There is no disclosed contract duration, no escalator, no take-or-pay, no minimum volume and no backlog anywhere in ten years of 20-Fs.

business-explainer · business-model · ↩ back to dossier

Company

filingsupports
We price our packaging services and electronic manufacturing services, taking into account the actual costs, with reference to prevailing market prices. We price our testing services primarily on the basis of the amount of time, measured in central processing unit seconds, taken by the automated testing equipment to execute the test programs specific to the products being tested, as well as the cost of the equipment, with reference to prevailing market prices. Prices for our packaging, testing and electronic manufacturing services are confirmed at the time orders are received from customers, which is typically several weeks before delivery.
— Advanced Semiconductor Engineering, Inc. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2016, 2017-04-21
filingsupports ✦ best
We price our packaging services and EMS by considering the actual costs and prevailing market prices. We price our testing services primarily on the basis of the amount of time, measured in central processing unit seconds, taken by the automated testing equipment to execute the test programs specific to the products being tested, as well as the cost of the equipment, with additional consideration of prevailing market prices. Prices for our packaging, testing, and EMS are confirmed at the time orders are received from customers, which is typically several weeks before delivery.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
The Group's duration of contracts with customers is expected to be one year or less, and the consideration from contracts with customers is included in transaction price and, therefore, can apply the practical expedient that not to disclose the performance obligations including (i) the aggregate amount of the transaction price allocated to the performance obligations that are not fully satisfied or have partially completed at the end of the reporting period, and (ii) the expected timing for recognition of revenue.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
We cannot ensure that these customers or any other customers will continue to place orders with us in the future at the same levels as in past periods. The loss of one or more of our significant customers, or reduced orders by any one of them, and our inability to replace these customers or make up for such orders, could adversely affect our revenues and profitability. In addition, we have in the past reduced, and may in the future be requested to reduce, our prices to limit the level of order cancellations. Any price reduction would likely reduce our margins and profitability.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingcontradicts
The Group enters into long-term agreements with customers and receives advance payments, which are recorded under other current liabilities and other liabilities. Under these agreements, the Group is obligated to sell products or provide services to customers.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingqualifies
The Group entered into long-term purchase agreements of materials and supplies with multiple suppliers. The relative minimum purchase quantity is specified in the agreements. ... The Group entered into long-term corporate power purchase agreements with suppliers. The relative fulfillment period, quantity and price are specified in the agreements.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
In addition, semiconductor businesses are capital intensive and require significant investment in expensive equipment manufactured by a limited number of vendors. The market for semiconductor equipment is characterized by intense demand, limited supply, and long delivery cycles. Our operations and expansion plans depend on our ability to obtain a significant amount of such equipment from a limited number of vendors. From time to time, we have also leased equipment. We have no binding supply agreements with any of our vendors and acquire our equipment on a purchase order basis, which exposes us to changing market conditions and other substantial risks.
— ASE Technology Holding Co., Ltd. Form 20-F for fiscal year 2025, 2026-04-01

Peer: Amkor Technology

filingsupports
Our packaging and test business does not typically operate with any material backlog. Our quarterly net sales from packaging and test services are substantially dependent upon our customers’ demand in that quarter. Generally, our customers do not commit to purchase any significant amount of packaging or test services or provide us with binding forecasts of demand for packaging and test services for any future period, in any material amount. In addition, we sometimes experience double booking by customers, and our customers often reduce, cancel or delay their purchases of packaging and test services for a variety of reasons, including industry-wide, customer-specific and Amkor-specific reasons.
— Amkor Technology, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20
filingsupports
We are a large buyer of gold and other commodity materials, including substrates and copper. The prices of gold and other commodities used in our business fluctuate. Historically, we have been able to partially offset the effect of commodity price increases through price adjustments to some customers and changes in our product designs that reduce the material content and cost, such as the use of shorter, thinner gold wire and migration to copper wire. However, we typically do not have long-term contracts that permit us to impose price adjustments, and market conditions may limit our ability to do so.
— Amkor Technology, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20
filingsupports
Our operations are characterized by high fixed costs and the absence of any material backlog. Our profitability depends in part not only on pricing levels for our packaging and test services but also on the efficient utilization of our human resources and packaging and test equipment. Increases or decreases in our capacity utilization can significantly affect gross margins.
— Amkor Technology, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20

Peer: ChipMOS Technologies

We also resumed our focus on our business with smaller customers or customers who do not place orders on a regular basis.

Our operations, in particular our testing operations and leading-edge advanced packaging, are characterized by relatively high fixed costs. ... Our profitability depends not only on the pricing levels for our services or products, but also on utilization rates for our machinery and equipment, commonly referred to as “capacity utilization rates.” Increases or decreases in our capacity utilization rates can significantly affect gross margins since the unit cost generally decreases as fixed costs are spread over a larger number of units. In periods of low demand, we experience relatively low capacity utilization rates in our operations, which lead to reduced margins.

earnings callsupports
As a note, during this year, we see our depreciation rising faster than revenues as a result of our ongoing investments in LEAP. The granularity of our OS and full-process LEAP equipment differs from our more traditional advanced packaging lines. Our LEAP lines must be installed at scale and together as a full set of differing machinery instead of small incremental units like wirebonders and testers. This results in our LEAP lines taking a greater amount of time to bring up and tune when compared with more traditional packaging capacities. Currently, our full-process LEAP lines are in the midst of tuning and qualification. As a result, we will continue to see gradually increasing depreciation without significant amounts of associated revenue during the tuning and qualification period.
— Kenneth Hsiang, ASE Technology Holding Q1 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-04-29

Since a substantial portion of the costs at our factories is fixed, there tends to be a strong relationship between our revenue levels and gross margin. Accordingly, relatively modest increases or decreases in revenue can have a significant effect on margin and on labor and other manufacturing costs as a percentage of revenue, depending on product mix, utilization, foreign currency exchange rate movements and seasonality. We have expanded our business in advanced packaging, which tends to have higher material costs than our other products. As we continue to increase production of these higher material cost products, there could be an impact on our profitability, depending on overall utilization.
— Amkor Technology, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20

Company

filingsupports ✦ best
We price our packaging services and EMS by considering the actual costs and prevailing market prices. We price our testing services primarily on the basis of the amount of time, measured in central processing unit seconds, taken by the automated testing equipment to execute the test programs specific to the products being tested, as well as the cost of the equipment, with additional consideration of prevailing market prices. Prices for our packaging, testing, and EMS are confirmed at the time orders are received from customers, which is typically several weeks before delivery.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
Our operations, in particular our testing operations, are characterized by relatively high fixed costs. We expect to continue to incur substantial depreciation and other expenses, especially from our acquisitions of packaging and testing equipment and facilities. Our profitability depends in part not only on absolute pricing levels for our products/services, but also on utilization rates on equipment, commonly referred to as “capacity utilization rates.” Increases or decreases in our capacity utilization rates could have a significant effect on gross margins since the unit cost of our products and/or services generally decreases as fixed costs are allocated over a larger number of units.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
Testing equipment is the most capital-intensive component of the testing process. We generally seek to purchase testers from different suppliers with similar functionality and acquire the ability to test a variety of different semiconductors. We purchase testers from major international manufacturers, primarily Teradyne, Inc., Tokyo Electron Limited, and Advantest Corporation. ... As of January 31, 2026, we operated an aggregate of 7,456 testers.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-04-01
filingqualifies
In addition to purchasing testers, we also lease a portion of our testers, which we believe allows us to better manage our capacity utilization rates and cash flows. Since leased testers can be replaced with more advanced testers upon the expiration of the lease, we believe that these leases have enabled us to improve our capacity utilization rates by allowing us to better align our capacity with changes in equipment technology and the needs of our customers.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-04-01
filingqualifies
For the majority of our testing equipment, we typically base our purchases on prior discussions with our customers about their forecast requirements. The balance consists of testing equipment on consignment from customers, which is dedicated exclusively to the testing of these customers’ specific products.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
Test programs, which consist of the software that drives the testing of specific semiconductors, are written for a specific testing platform. We sometimes perform test program conversions that enable us to test semiconductors on multiple test platforms. This portability between testers enables us to allocate semiconductors tested across our available test capabilities and thereby improve capacity utilization rates.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
In particular, the capacity utilization rates for our testing equipment are more severely affected during an industry downturn as a result of a decrease in outsourcing demand from IDMs, which typically maintain larger in-house testing capacity than in-house packaging capacity.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-04-01

The primary equipment used in the testing process includes testers, handlers and probers. Handlers are used to transfer individual or small groups of packaged ICs to a tester. Test equipment is generally a more capital-intensive activity than packaging, and test equipment tends to have longer delivery lead times than most types of packaging equipment. We focus our capital expenditures on standardized tester platforms to maximize test equipment utilization where possible. In some cases, our customers will consign test equipment to us.
— Amkor Technology 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20
filingsupports
The silicon wafer is generally consigned from our customer. We generally do not take ownership of the customer consigned wafer, and title and risk of loss remains with the customer for these materials. Test materials constitute a very small portion of our total test cost.
— Amkor Technology 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20
filingcontradicts
We recognize revenue over time as services are rendered because our services create or enhance the customer’s wafer. We utilize an input method (cost incurred plus estimated margin) to determine the amount of revenue to recognize for in-process, but incomplete, customer orders at a reporting date. During the period of providing our services, we generally do not control or take ownership of customers’ wafers, nor do we include the cost of the wafer in our cost calculations.
— Amkor Technology 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-20

Over FY2018–FY2025 wafer probing compounded at 19.9% a year while final testing compounded at 5.8%. The shift toward probe did not begin with AI; it began in 2018 and AI accelerated it.

We price our packaging services and EMS by considering the actual costs and prevailing market prices. We price our testing services primarily on the basis of the amount of time, measured in central processing unit seconds, taken by the automated testing equipment to execute the test programs specific to the products being tested, as well as the cost of the equipment, with additional consideration of prevailing market prices. Prices for our packaging, testing, and EMS are confirmed at the time orders are received from customers, which is typically several weeks before delivery.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F, FY2025, 2026-04-01

The semiconductor industry is characterized by a general trend toward declining prices for products and services of a given technology over time. In addition, during periods of intense competition and adverse conditions in the semiconductor industry, the rate of this decline may be more rapid than in other years. The average selling prices of our packaging and testing services have experienced sharp declines during such periods as a result of intense price competition from other market participants that attempt to maintain high-capacity utilization levels in the face of reduced demand.
— ASE Technology Holding FY2025 Form 20-F, 2026-04-01
filingsupports
We price our packaging services and EMS by considering the actual costs and prevailing market prices. We price our testing services primarily on the basis of the amount of time, measured in central processing unit seconds, taken by the automated testing equipment to execute the test programs specific to the products being tested, as well as the cost of the equipment, with additional consideration of prevailing market prices. Prices for our packaging, testing, and EMS are confirmed at the time orders are received from customers, which is typically several weeks before delivery.
— ASE Technology Holding FY2025 Form 20-F, 2026-04-01
filingsupports

During the quarter, we saw a material move in the NT dollar to U.S. dollar exchange rate. The NT dollar moved from an average exchange rate of NTD 32.8 to NTD 31.2 per U.S. dollar strengthening by 4.9%, with our revenues generally based in U.S. dollars, and a large percentage of our ATM expenses being NT dollar based. The foreign exchange fluctuation was detrimental to our financial performance. ... we estimate that for every percentage point appreciation of the NT dollar relative to the U.S. dollar, we see a corresponding 0.3 percentage point negative impact to our gross and operating margins at the holding company level and a 0.45 percentage point negative impact to margins at the ATM level. Using this simplified approach, we can estimate that on a sequential basis, foreign exchange had impacts to our holding company and ATM margins of 1.5 and 2.2 percentage points, respectively.

filingsupports
Our operations, in particular our testing operations and leading-edge advanced packaging, are characterized by relatively high fixed costs. We expect to continue to incur substantial depreciation and other expenses in connection with our acquisitions of equipment and facilities. Our profitability depends not only on the pricing levels for our services or products, but also on utilization rates for our machinery and equipment, commonly referred to as capacity utilization rates. Increases or decreases in our capacity utilization rates can significantly affect gross margins since the unit cost generally decreases as fixed costs are spread over a larger number of units. In periods of low demand, we experience relatively low capacity utilization rates in our operations, which lead to reduced margins.
— ASE Technology Holding FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 3.D Risk Factors, 2026-04-01

**Accuracy is not degrading — the bias is widening.** In the qualitative era (3Q20–1Q24, 15 quarters) the beats were mostly small and unquantifiable. The gap between what management publishes and what it delivers is getting wider, not tighter.

Every OSAT-class year with capex/D&A ≥1.6x (46 events, 39 with a three-year forward outcome): ... | ROIC in the surge year | 1.7% | 3.1% | **5.5%** | 7.4% | 9.3% | | ROIC three years later | 2.0% | 2.6% | **4.3%** | 6.6% | 9.0% | | Δ ROIC, surge → +3 (pp) | −4.7 | −2.4 | **−0.8** | +1.1 | +3.2 | | Δ gross margin, surge → +3 (pp) | −8.2 | −4.3 | **−2.1** | +0.8 | +3.7 | | Revenue CAGR over the next 3 years | +6.1% | +8.7% | **+14.7%** | +29.3% | +39.1% | ... ROIC was higher three years later in **15 of 39 cases (38%)**. The capex buys revenue — a median +14.7%/yr for three years, which is well above the class's unconditional +10.8% — and does not buy returns. The median outcome is more revenue at a slightly lower margin on a much larger capital base.
— ASX deep-dive workpaper, expectations-baserate, 2026-08-03
websupports
Persistent 25%+ capital intensity is normal in this industry and it is not associated with high returns anywhere except TSMC. The four companies with the longest high-capex records outside TSMC — KYEC, Tongfu, Huatian, UMC — earned median ROIC of 3.0–7.0% while doing it. Amkor, the one OSAT that has never run capex above 22% of revenue, has the highest peak ROIC in the class.
— ASX deep-dive workpaper, expectations-baserate, 2026-08-03
websupports
ROIIC (Δ NOPAT / Δ invested capital): - FY2016 → FY2025: **+7.1%** (ΔNOPAT +NT$20.4bn on ΔIC +NT$289bn) - FY2019 → FY2025: **+14.3%** ... - FY2022 → FY2025: **−25.1%** (ΔNOPAT −NT$22.9bn on ΔIC +NT$91bn) ... The last three years put NT$91bn of new capital in and took NT$23bn of NOPAT out. ... But the record as it stands does not show incremental returns above any plausible cost of capital.

We make significant investments in equipment and facilities in order to service the demand of our customers. The amount of our capital expenditures depends on several factors, including the performance of our business, our assessment of future industry and customer demand, our capacity utilization levels and availability, advances in technology, our liquidity position and the availability of financing. Our ongoing capital expenditure requirements may strain our cash and liquidity, and, in periods when we are expanding our capital base, we expect that depreciation expense and factory operating expenses associated with capital expenditures to increase production capacity will put downward pressure on our gross profit, at least in the near term.

Gross margin decreased to 14.0% in 2025 compared to 14.8% in 2024. Gross margin for 2025 was also constrained by the ramp up of production at the Vietnam Facility, which is in the early stages of high-volume manufacturing. ... Our operations are characterized by high fixed costs and the absence of any material backlog. In addition, our fixed operating costs have increased as a result of capital expenditures for capacity expansion. The anticipated customer demand for which we have made capital investments may not materialize, and our sales may not adequately cover fixed costs, resulting in reduced profit levels or even significant losses.

The placement is therefore precise: consensus does not require ASE to do something the industry has never done. It requires ASE to sustain its own best-in-class capital productivity, at roughly 3.5x the annual capex it has ever deployed, with no year of degradation. In the class, capital productivity degrades as programs scale — KYEC's ratio of 0.16 and Powertech's 0.32 are what heavy, sustained, test-weighted programs produce, and ASE's own capex mix is shifting toward test (40% of the FY2026 equipment plan; testing capex was 71.3% of Testing segment revenue in FY2025).

**The older revenue-per-capex rule.** Analysts on the Q3 2023 and Q3 2024 calls referenced a management rule of thumb of roughly NT$1.1 of revenue per NT$1 of capex for flip chip. Over FY2016–FY2022 cumulative capex was NT$405bn and revenue rose NT$396bn — a ratio of 0.98x, broadly consistent. Over FY2023–FY2025 cumulative capex was NT$298bn and revenue FELL NT$25bn from the 2022 peak.
— ASX deep-dive workpaper, capital-allocation, 2026-08-03
webcontradicts
**The one hard forward datapoint on conversion.** LEAP (leading-edge advanced packaging) service revenue was US$0.6bn in 2024 and US$1.6bn in 2025 (13% of ATM revenue); 2026 is "tracking ahead of our prior quarter guidance of USD 3.5 billion" and management is "aiming to double our LEAP revenue in 2027" (Q2 2026 call). Against FY2025 + FY2026 capex of US$5.5bn + US$10.5bn = US$16bn, an incremental ≈US$6.4bn of annual LEAP revenue by 2027 implies roughly 2.5x of capex per NT$1 of incremental annual revenue, versus ≈1x historically.

Wire bonders connect the input/output terminals on the silicon die using extremely fine gold or copper wire to leads on leadframes or substrates. Typically, a wire bonder may be used, with minor modifications, for the packaging of different products. As of January 31, 2026, we operated an aggregate of 25,001 wire bonders. For the packaging of certain types of substrate-based packages, die bonders are used in place of wire bonders. The number of bonders at a given facility is commonly used as a measure of the packaging capacity of the facility.

Our Mainstream Products use wirebond interconnect technology to connect a die to a leadframe or substrate package carrier and include leadframe packages, substrate-based wirebond packages and micro-electro-mechanical systems (“MEMS”) packages. Leadframe Packages: Leadframe packages use wirebond or flip chip technology to connect a die to a leadframe package carrier. Leadframe packages are used in many electronic devices and remain the most practical and cost-effective solution for many low to medium pin count analog and mixed signal applications.
— Amkor Technology 10-K, FY2025, 2026-02-20
filingqualifies
We are a large buyer of gold and other commodity materials, including substrates and copper. The prices of gold and other commodities used in our business fluctuate. Historically, we have been able to partially offset the effect of commodity price increases through price adjustments to some customers and changes in our product designs that reduce the material content and cost, such as the use of shorter, thinner gold wire and migration to copper wire. However, we typically do not have long-term contracts that permit us to impose price adjustments, and market conditions may limit our ability to do so.

We define leading-edge advanced packages as packaging technologies that incorporate redistribution layer (RDL) processes. By leveraging RDL processes, embedded integration, and 2.5D and 3D technologies, these packaging solutions facilitate unprecedented innovation in integrating multiple chips within a single package. Notable technologies include ASEH's FOWLP (Fan-Out Wafer-Level Package), high-density RDL-based Fanout Package-on-Package (FOPoP), Fanout Chip-on-Substrate (FOCoS), Fanout Chip-on-Substrate-Bridge (FOCoS-Bridge), Fanout System-in-Package (FOSiP), Through Silicon Via (TSV)-based 2.5D and 3D IC, along with Co-Packaged Optics processing capabilities.

2.5D/3D include multiple IC within the same package. In a 2.5D structure, two or more active semiconductor chips are placed side-by-side on a silicon interposer to achieve extremely high die-to-die interconnect density. In a 3D structure, active chips are integrated through die stacking to achieve shortest interconnects and smallest package footprint.

Amkor is a global leader in advanced semiconductor packaging and test technologies, enabling next-generation devices across key markets such as high-performance computing (“HPC”) and artificial intelligence (“AI”), automotive, IoT and mobile communications. Our technology leadership encompasses areas such as high density fan-out (“HDFO”), including SWIFT and S-Connect packaging technologies, 2.5D integration, advanced flip chip, fine pitch bumping, wafer-level processing and advanced system-in-package (“SiP”) solutions, which support the industry's drive toward smaller form factors, higher integration, improved performance and lower power consumption.
— Amkor Technology FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business — Overview, 2026-02-20
filingsupports
HDFO can either replace the laminate substrate with a thinner structure or reduce the complexity of the substrate by housing the dense interconnects and bridges in the redistribution layer structure, allowing for a less expensive substrate that provides a high level of performance with a balanced cost structure. HDFO solutions enable high performance in a compact form factor that combines tiled processors, memory, I/O (input/output) die and other peripheral ICs.
— Amkor Technology FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business — Wafer-level Package Products, 2026-02-20
filingsupports
SiPh and CPO allow for the integration of fiberoptic interconnects both within a package and within the server architecture to reduce power consumption, heat generation and improve performance. Amkor has supported previous generations of SiPh packaging needs and is aligned with the market to support its future needs, through chip on wafer, chip on chip and other advanced packaging solutions.
— Amkor Technology FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business — Wafer-level Package Products, 2026-02-20
filingsupports
Wafer-level and panel-level packaging: Innovating 2D and 3D wafer-level chip-scale packages and WLFO packages are increasingly the preferred package type for many applications in IoT and mobile devices, including processors, power management integrated circuits (“PMICs”), display drivers and antenna package products. We believe that our development of Panel Level Fan Out (“PLFO”) technology will permit greater economies of scale for fan-out package devices manufactured on a panel versus wafer basis.
— Amkor Technology FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business — Research and Development, 2026-02-20

Test programs, which consist of the software that drives the testing of specific semiconductors, are written for a specific testing platform. We sometimes perform test program conversions that enable us to test semiconductors on multiple test platforms. This portability between testers enables us to allocate semiconductors tested across our available test capabilities and thereby improve capacity utilization rates. In cases where a customer has specified testing equipment that was not widely applicable to other products that we test, we have required the customer to furnish the equipment on a consignment basis.

filingsupports

filingsupports
Our operations, in particular our testing operations and leading-edge advanced packaging, are characterized by relatively high fixed costs. We expect to continue to incur substantial depreciation and other expenses in connection with our acquisitions of equipment and facilities. Our profitability depends not only on the pricing levels for our services or products, but also on utilization rates for our machinery and equipment, commonly referred to as “capacity utilization rates.” Increases or decreases in our capacity utilization rates can significantly affect gross margins since the unit cost generally decreases as fixed costs are spread over a larger number of units.

filingqualifies
A package technology that stacks a die on top of a probed known good die to integrate ASIC and memory (flash, SRAM, and DDR) into one package and interconnects them with wire bonding and molding. This technology suffers from known good die issues (i.e., one bad die will ruin the entire module). Rework is also not an option in hybrid packages.
— ASE Technology Holding FY2025 Form 20-F — advanced packaging table, “Hybrid (Flip Chip and Wire Bonding)” row (49-608 leads), 2026-04-01
filingsupports
In a 3D structure, active chips are integrated through die stacking to achieve shortest interconnects and smallest package footprint.
— ASE Technology Holding FY2025 Form 20-F — advanced packaging table, 2.5D and 3D IC row, 2026-04-01
filingqualifies
Our research and development efforts in testing have focused primarily on developing advanced package testing solutions for HPC, mm Wave, SiP, silicon photonics, and optical sensor modules; characterization of semiconductors, layout design and electrical simulation for high-frequency test board and developing software of parametric test data analysis.
— ASE Technology Holding FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 5 — Research and Development, Testing, 2026-04-01

filingsupports
A significant area of focus is on integrated multi-die solutions, including 2.5D, SWIFT and S-Connect. ASE sits at the layer with the highest physical leverage over throughput and the lowest capture of the end price. It converts a bare die worth thousands of dollars into a saleable part for a fee that is a low single-digit percentage of that part's selling price, and it does so without owning the die.

filingsupports ✦ best
In the case of semiconductor packaging, the cost of the silicon die, typically the most costly component of the packaged semiconductor, is usually not reflected in our costs (or revenues) since it is generally supplied by our customers on a consignment basis.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F FY2025 — Item 5 Operating Results, 'Pricing and Revenue Mix', 2026-04-01
filingsupports
The principal raw materials used in our packaging processes are interconnect materials such as leadframes and substrates, gold wire, and molding compound. The silicon die, which is the functional unit of the semiconductor to be packaged, is supplied in the form of silicon wafers. Each silicon wafer contains a number of identical dies. We receive the wafers from customers or foundries on a consignment basis. Consequently, we generally do not incur inventory costs relating to the silicon wafers used in our packaging process.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F FY2025 — Item 4.B Business Overview, 'Raw Materials and Suppliers — Packaging', 2026-04-01
filingsupports
We price our packaging services and EMS by considering the actual costs and prevailing market prices. We price our testing services primarily on the basis of the amount of time, measured in central processing unit seconds, taken by the automated testing equipment to execute the test programs specific to the products being tested, as well as the cost of the equipment, with additional consideration of prevailing market prices. Prices for our packaging, testing, and EMS are confirmed at the time orders are received from customers, which is typically several weeks before delivery.
— ASE Technology Holding 20-F FY2025 — Item 4.B Business Overview, 'Pricing', 2026-04-01
